During that time, I lost all interest in the activities I used to enjoy and just couldn't bring myself to care about anything.\n\nDoctor: Did these periods of elevated mood and energy occur before, after, or both before and after the major depressive episode you described, and were there any noticeable patterns or triggers to these mood shifts?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure about the exact order of when they happened or what triggers them. I just know that the high-energy period lasted for about 11 months, while the depressed period went on for about a year and two months.\n\nDoctor: Can you try to recall if the major depressive episode and the high-energy period ever overlapped, or if one clearly started after the other had ended, to help clarify the pattern of your mood episodes?\n\nPatient: I'm not quite sure about the exact timing or whether those two periods overlapped. It is just really difficult for me to recall the precise sequence of when they started and ended.\n\nDoctor: During these episodes of feeling incredibly high or extremely irritable, have you ever experienced any break from reality, such as hearing voices or seeing things that aren't there?\n\nPatient: No, I have never experienced anything like that. I don't hear voices or see things that aren't there, even when my mood is at its most extreme.\n\nDoctor: Can you distinguish between the periods of elevated mood where you felt extremely energetic and those where you felt extremely irritable, and did either of these types of episodes significantly impact your daily life or relationships?"

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
They aren't really separate, as my mood shifts unpredictably between feeling euphoric and full of energy to suddenly becoming highly irritable. These episodes definitely impact my life because when I'm highly energetic, I take on way too much work and become overly social. This ends up being exhausting and really disruptive to my daily routine and relationships.

{"candidates": ["Bipolar II Disorder"], "note": "The patient's description of experiencing periods of elevated mood and energy, as well as major depressive episodes, aligns with the criteria for Bipolar II Disorder. The patient's report of never having a manic episode that lasted at least 7 days or required hospitalization, but instead experiencing hypomanic episodes (periods of elevated mood and energy that lasted longer than 4 days), supports this diagnosis. Additionally, the patient's denial of any psychotic symptoms, such as hearing voices or seeing things that aren't there, reduces the likelihood of Bipolar I Disorder or other psychotic disorders. The presence of both hypomanic and major depressive episodes, without clear evidence of full manic episodes, makes Bipolar II Disorder the most likely diagnosis at this point. Bipolar I Disorder seems less likely due to the absence of clear manic episodes or psychotic features.", "is_final": false}

Instead, I had a period of about 11 months where I felt the opposite—extremely energetic, constantly on the go, and highly active both at work and socially, even though my mood was elevated and very irritable.\n\nDoctor: During those 11 months, did you experience any periods of at least 4 consecutive days where you felt elevated or irritable, and had increased energy, but were still able to function in your daily life and didn't necessarily need to be hospitalized?\n\nPatient: Yes, definitely. During those 11 months, I had several periods lasting much longer than four days where my energy was overflowing and I felt extremely elevated or irritable. Even though I was hyperactive and constantly on the go, I was still able to get through my daily routine and never needed to be hospitalized.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any major depressive episodes, lasting at least two weeks, where you felt persistently sad, empty, or lost interest in activities you once enjoyed, in addition to these periods of elevated mood and energy?\n\nPatient: Yes, I definitely have. I went through a very long period of about a year and two months where I felt constantly depressed, sad, and completely empty inside. During that time, I lost all interest in the activities I used to enjoy and just couldn't bring myself to care about anything.\n\nDoctor: Did these periods of elevated mood and energy occur before, after, or both before and after the major depressive episode you described, and were there any noticeable patterns or triggers to these mood shifts?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure about the exact order of when they happened or what triggers them. I just know that the high-energy period lasted for about 11 months, while the depressed period went on for about a year and two months.\n\nDoctor: Can you try to recall if the major depressive episode and the high-energy period ever overlapped, or if one clearly started after the other had ended, to help clarify the pattern of your mood episodes?\n\nPatient: I'm not quite sure about the exact timing or whether those two periods overlapped. It is just really difficult for me to recall the precise sequence of when they started and ended.\n\nDoctor: During these episodes of feeling incredibly high or extremely irritable, have you ever experienced any break from reality, such as hearing voices or seeing things that aren't there?\n\nPatient: No, I have never experienced anything like that. I don't hear voices or see things that aren't there, even when my mood is at its most extreme.\n\nDoctor: Can you distinguish between the periods of elevated mood where you felt extremely energetic and those where you felt extremely irritable, and did either of these types of episodes significantly impact your daily life or relationships?\n\nPatient: They aren't really separate, as my mood shifts unpredictably between feeling euphoric and full of energy to suddenly becoming highly irritable. These episodes definitely impact my life because when I'm highly energetic, I take on way too much work and become overly social. This ends up being exhausting and really disruptive to my daily routine and relationships.\n\nDoctor: How have these episodes of elevated mood and energy, as well as the depressive episodes, affected your overall functioning, relationships, and daily life, and are you currently experiencing any distress or impairment due to these symptoms?\n\nPatient: These alternating episodes have caused huge disruptions in my life, as I'd become overly social and take on too many work projects during my high-energy phases. In contrast, during my depressive periods, I completely withdrew from everyone, lost all motivation to work, and struggled with severe sleep disturbances. This constant back-and-forth has made it incredibly difficult to maintain a stable daily routine and has caused me a lot of distress."
